\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}$}}}}}}}}}\neq 0.

Hence the
(v^{+})\cdot\textsl{p}_{\text{even}}
are nonzero. It is also clear that they are mutually distinct (for example by looking at quantum degree). Moreover, since any further crossing also commutes with
\textsl{p}_{\text{even}}
, we have
(v^{+}\textsl{p}_{\text{even}})\cdot\sigma=0
. In this way we obtain many mutually distinct ‘highest weight’ (in the sense of being killed by all crossings) vectors. More generally

\{(v^{+})\cdot P:P\in\mathbb{C}[\textsl{p}_{2},\textsl{p}_{4},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{p}_{\theta}]\}

are all mutually distinct nonzero highest weight vectors. Note that a basis for this lattice is given by

\{(v^{+})\cdot\textsl{p}_{\text{even partition}}\},

where an even partition means a partition whose parts are all even.

Now consider

\raisebox{-1.49994pt}{\scalebox{1.32}[1.4]{$\nicefrac{{(\mathfrak{j}^{\theta})^{k}}}{{(\mathfrak{j}^{\theta})^{k+1}}}$}}\otimes_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}}\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}.

This is a semisimple
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}
-representation. Under the identification above, inside of it are distinct highest weight vectors
(v^{+})\cdot\textsl{p}_{\lambda}
for even partitions
\lambda
of length
k
. By the linear independence of these vectors and the semisimplicity of the action over the nilHecke
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}
, we know that, when
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}
is allowed to act on them, they each generate a copy of
L_{\theta}(\theta)
(appropriately shifted). This gives
\dim\mathbb{C}[\textsl{p}_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{p}_{2n}]_{\deg_{\text{sym}}=k}
many copies of
L_{\theta}(\theta)
, each shifted by
q^{-n}
, in the
k
-th layer of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
. Together, across all
k
, these would have dimension equal to
q^{-n}l_{\theta}(\theta)\dim\mathbb{C}[\textsl{p}_{2},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{p}_{2n}]
, which is already the full dimension of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
. Hence this must be everything. We immediately get the desired filtration with quotients of the claimed dimension.

And in particular,
v^{+}
generates all of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
via the action of dots, and since the dimension of
\mathbb{C}[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]/\langle\textsl{p}_{\text{odd}}\rangle
is already the correct dimension of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
, we conclude it must be the case that

\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}\cong\mathbb{C}[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]/\langle\textsl{p}_{\text{odd}}\rangle,

where the isomorphism is via the action on
v^{+}
.

Let us quickly check that
\mathbb{C}[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]/\langle\textsl{p}_{\text{odd}}\rangle
is indeed the correct dimension. By PIE, this dimension is

\displaystyle\dim\mathbb{C}[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]/\langle\textsl{p}_{\text{odd}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-q^{-2})^{\theta}}\Big(1-q^{-2}-q^{-6}-\cdots-q^{-2(\theta-1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+q^{-(2+6)}+q^{-(2+10)}+\cdots+q^{-(2+2(\theta-1))}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+q^{-(6+10)}+q^{-(6+14)}+\cdots+q^{-(6+2(\theta-1))}

\displaystyle\hskip 110.00017pt\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt+\cdots+q^{-(2(\theta-3)+2(\theta-1))}

\displaystyle\hskip 120.00018pt-\cdots\Big),

which can be collapsed to

\displaystyle\dim\mathbb{C}[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]/\langle\textsl{p}_{\text{odd}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-q^{-2})(1-q^{-6})\cdots(1-q^{-2(\theta-1)})}{(1-q^{-2})^{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-q^{-2})(1-q^{-6})\cdots(1-q^{-4n+2})}{(1-q^{-2})^{2n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+q^{-2}+q^{-4})(1+q^{-2}+\cdots+q^{-8})\cdots(1+q^{-2}+\cdots+q^{-4n+4})}{(1-q^{-2})^{n}}

\displaystyle=q^{(-4)+(-8)+\cdots+(-4n+4)}\frac{(1+q^{2}+q^{4})\cdots(1+\cdots+q^{4n-4})}{(1-q^{-2})^{n}}

\displaystyle=q^{-4\binom{n}{2}}\frac{(1+q^{2}+q^{4})\cdots(1+\cdots+q^{4n-4})}{(1-q^{-2})^{n}}.

Note that the
q^{-4\binom{n}{2}}
cancels out with the degree of the vector
v^{+}
, and the remaining part is recognizable from the earlier theorem statement on the dimension of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
. This concludes the proof.∎

BGG

The results of the previous subsection, together with Theorem
, show that there is a resolution of
L_{0}
at
t=0
by extensions of proper standard modules. This is the ‘BGG resolution’ of the one-dimensional simple.

Indeed, apply
to
M=L_{0}
, which lies in the even block, namely
\vartheta=0
. Then the spectral sequence in Theorem
has first page terms
E_{1}^{p,q}
nonzero only when
-(p+q)=\frac{1}{2}(\vartheta-2p)=-p
, thanks to our result on the concentration of standard Ext groups. In other words
q=0
on
E_{1}
and
p\leq 0
, i.e. the first page is concentrated on the negative
x
-axis:
p
q
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
⋯

Then, as
L_{0}
is concentrated in homological degree 0 (so that
E_{\infty}^{p,q}
must be
L_{0}
sitting at the origin), it is clear that the spectral sequence must converge after one step, i.e.
E_{1}^{\bullet,0}
is a resolution of
L_{0}
. Moreover, recalling that
\Delta(2n)=\Delta_{2n}^{\overline{l_{2n}(2n)}}={\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq 2n}e^{2n}
has an obvious right
{\text{N}\cal B}^{2n}
-action, we have

E_{1}^{-n,0}=\Delta_{2n}^{\overline{l_{2n}(2n)}}\otimes_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{2n}}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\Delta_{2n}^{\overline{l_{2n}(2n)}},L_{0})^{*},

where the structure of the standard Ext group has already been determined. Hence each term of this resolution is an extension of proper standard modules whose structure is completely understood. So in conclusion we have shown:

\vbox{}\left.\vbox{\vbox to74.51695pt{\vfil}}\right[\vbox{\vskip-7.5pt\begin{theorem}At parameter $t=0$, the 1-dimensional simple $L_{0}$ has a BGG resolution
$$\cdots\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{-n}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{-(n-1)}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\cdots\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{0}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow L_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0$$where the terms have character
$$\chi(C^{-n}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0}))=\frac{q^{-n}}{(1-q^{-4})(1-q^{-8})\cdots(1-q^{-4n})}\chi(\overline{\Delta}_{2n})$$and admit filtrations $C^{-n}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0})=\text{F}^{0}_{\text{BGG}}\supset\text{F}^{1}_{\text{BGG}}\supset\cdots$ such that
$$\operatorname{gr}^{k}C^{-n}_{\text{BGG}}(L_{0})=\overline{\Delta}_{2n}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}q^{-n}\mathbb{C}[\textsl{p}_{2},\textsl{p}_{4},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{p}_{2n}]_{\deg_{\text{sym}}=k},$$where $\deg_{\text{sym}}\textsl{p}_{i}=1$.\end{theorem}\vskip-6.0pt}

This provides a categorification of the character formula Equation (
1
) for
L_{0}
, thorough in the sense that a description of each
{\text{N}\cal B}
-module in the resolution is given.

Note that Theorem
provides a categorification via spectral sequences of the character formula (
1
) for all simples, even though in general we do not have a description of the module structure of each term. Indeed, plugging in
M=L_{2n+\vartheta}\in\mathsf{D}^{-}(\mathsf{Mod}^{\vartheta}\,{\text{N}\cal B})
for
\vartheta=0,1
, one obtains

\vbox{}\left.\vbox{\vbox to65.37805pt{\vfil}}\right[\vbox{\vskip-7.5pt\begin{theorem}There is a spectral sequence
$$E^{p,q}_{1}=\Delta(\vartheta-2p)\otimes_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\vartheta-2p}}\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{-(p+q)}(\Delta(\vartheta-2p),L_{2n+\vartheta})^{*}\>\;\implies\>\;E^{p,q}_{\infty}=\begin{cases}L_{2n+\vartheta}&p=q=0\\
0&\text{else}\end{cases};$$the terms $E^{p,q}_{1}$ of the first page are extensions of proper standard modules $\overline{\Delta}_{\vartheta-2p}$, and by the fact that simples and proper standards form bases of the Grothendieck group, this must categorify the character formula (\ref{eqn:bww}) in all cases.\end{theorem}\vskip-6.0pt}

7.
Proper Standard Ext Groups

In the previous section we have managed to compute the nilHecke structure of the standard Ext groups (or equivalently nilcohomology) because in this specific case of the nilBrauer we had a very good guess for the ‘highest weight vector’. In general this may be difficult to do, and in order to say more about the finer structure of the Ext groups
\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{\bullet}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0})^{*}=\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{\bullet}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}=H^{\bullet}({\text{N}\cal B}^{-}:L_{0})^{*}
one would first have to discuss a different Ext group, namely
\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{\bullet}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}
. (More generally one would consider
\operatorname{Ext}_{A}^{\bullet}(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\theta}\overline{\Delta}_{\lambda}^{\overline{l_{\lambda}(\theta)}},M)^{*}
.) As these are Ext groups out of proper standard modules, we shall call them “proper standard Ext groups”, or “small Ext groups”. Of course, independently of the question of determining the nilHecke structure on the standard Ext groups, these proper standard Ext groups are interesting for homological reasons. In some sense they are harder to handle. If one knows a reasonably informative filtration of projectives over the Cartan (e.g. a Jacobson/Loewy filtration), one can then get a spectral sequence whose first page are proper standard Ext groups and which converges to (the graded pieces of) standard Ext groups. The amount of information you can get out of this depends, of course, on how informative the starting filtration was. In our case we have such a filtration, but instead of computing the Cartan structure of the standard Ext groups by using this spectral sequence, we will work backwards using what we have proved earlier to obtain results about proper standard Ext groups.

Let us say a few words about the differences between standard and proper standard Ext groups. Big and small Vermas inherit different properties of actual standard modules from a highest weight context: while standard modules inherit the property of being freely generated from a single vector, proper standard modules
\overline{\Delta}_{\lambda}
inherit the property of containing exactly one copy of the simple
L_{\lambda}
in its JH series, with every other simple having
\mu\in\phi>\theta
(using lowest weight notation here). Other properties, such as BGG reciprocity and Ext vanishing, require both standard and proper standard objects to state. In particular proper standards lose the property of being freely generated on a single vector – that vector, say of
L_{\lambda}(\theta)
, has the additional stipulation of being killed by the Jacobson radical of the Cartan algebra
A^{\theta}
.

From the nilHecke over
\Gamma
to nilBrauer

Recall that the Cartan algebras
A^{\theta}
in our case are
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}=\Gamma[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta},\sigma_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\sigma_{\theta-1}]/\sim
, the nilHecke over the ground ring
\Gamma\cong\mathbb{C}[\textsl{q}_{1},\textsl{q}_{3},\cdots]
. As usual, this algebra is isomorphic to the matrix algebra over the symmetric polynomials in the
X
’s over
\Gamma
, which in turn is Morita-equivalent to just the ring of symmetric polynomials over
\Gamma
, which we denote

\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)\coloneqq\Gamma[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]^{S_{\theta}}=\Gamma[\textsl{e}_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{e}_{\theta}].

Note the usage of the slanted text font to denote symmetric polynomials
6
6

6

This is done in an effort to distinguish e.g. the elementary symmetric polynomials
\textsl{e}_{i}
from the idempotents
e^{\theta}
, or the Schur
q
-functions
\textsl{q}_{i}
from the quantum parameter
q
.
. This algebra has two natural gradings: the quantum grading, or the
q
-grading, in which
7
7

7

We have elected not to decorate the degree with a subscript here because this was the first degree one considered.

\deg\textsl{e}_{i}=2i,\hskip 20.00003pt\deg\textsl{q}_{i}=2i,

and the symmetric grading, in which

\deg_{\text{sym}}\textsl{e}_{i}=1,\hskip 20.00003pt\deg_{\text{sym}}\textsl{q}_{i}=1.

Note well that the (
q
-graded) algebra
\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)
has (up to grading shift) a unique simple module, namely the one-dimensional
\mathbb{C}
, appearing as the head. The Loewy filtration of this algebra as a module over itself, namely the projective indecomposable, is also very simple: it is the symmetric-degree filtration, given by

\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)=\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}\geq 0}\supset\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}\geq 1}\supset\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}\geq 2}\supset\cdots,

so that one visibly has that
\operatorname{Hed}\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)\cong\mathbb{C}
, as well as other things such as
\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}=1}\coloneqq\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}\geq 1}/\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}\geq 2}\cong\mathbb{C}\{\textsl{e}_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,\textsl{e}_{\theta},\textsl{q}_{1},\textsl{q}_{3},\cdots\}
, which has a (quantum-)dimension one can easily read off. For convenience, let

M_{\theta}(k)\coloneqq\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)_{\deg_{\text{sym}}=k}.

Note well that the
q
-dimension of this is

\dim M_{\theta}(k)=[t^{k}]\frac{1}{(1-tq^{-2})(1-tq^{-4})\cdots(1-tq^{-2\theta})}\prod_{0<i\equiv 2\mod 4}\frac{1}{1-tq^{-i}},

where the
[t^{k}]
in front indicates that we are taking the coefficient in front of
t^{k}
, and note
\prod_{0<i\equiv 2\mod 4}\frac{1}{1-tq^{-i}}=\dim\Gamma
.

As
\Sym_{\Gamma}(\theta)
is Morita-equivalent to
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}
, we then have that the projective indecomposable of
{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}
, namely
P_{\theta}(\theta)=q^{\binom{\theta}{2}}\Gamma[X_{1},\cdots\hskip-0.1pt,X_{\theta}]
, has a filtration
\text{F}=\text{F}_{\text{sym}}

P_{\theta}(\theta)=\text{F}^{0}P_{\theta}(\theta)\supset\text{F}^{1}P_{\theta}(\theta)\supset\text{F}^{2}P_{\theta}(\theta)\supset\cdots

in which the quotients, which are semisimple, are

\operatorname{gr}_{\text{F}}^{k}P_{\theta}(\theta)=L_{\theta}(\theta)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k).

Now we use the fact that
\jmath^{\theta}_{!}
is exact to obtain from this a filtration for
\Delta_{\theta}
in which the quotients are
\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k)
:

\Delta_{\theta}=\text{F}^{0}\Delta_{\theta}\supset\text{F}^{1}\Delta_{\theta}\supset\text{F}^{2}\Delta_{\theta}\supset\cdots,

with

\operatorname{gr}_{\text{F}}^{k}\Delta_{\theta}=\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k).

The spectral sequence

The idea now is to compute
\operatorname{Ext}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}
in terms of
\operatorname{Ext}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}
by using the filtration above to obtain a spectral sequence.

From the filtration of
\Delta
’s by
\overline{\Delta}
’s above, one obtains a spectral sequence going from proper standard Ext groups to standard Ext groups. To be more precise, consider the complex
\Hom_{{\text{N}\cal B}}({\mathop{\square}},Q^{\bullet})^{*}
for an injective resolution
L_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow Q^{\bullet}
of
L_{0}
. This is a covariant functor which is exact, and so each

0\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k+1}\Delta_{\theta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k}\Delta_{\theta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0,

or rather each

0\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k+1}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0,

under this functor gives rise to

0\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k+1}\Hom_{\text{N}\cal B}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},Q^{\bullet})^{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\text{F}^{k}\Hom_{\text{N}\cal B}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},Q^{\bullet})^{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Hom_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k),Q^{\bullet})^{*}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0.

So there is a filtration on the complex
\Hom_{\text{N}\cal B}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},Q^{\bullet})^{*}
whose associated graded is
\Hom_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},Q^{\bullet})^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(k)
, and by the theory of spectral sequences we have a spectral sequence

E_{1}^{p,q}=\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M_{\theta}(p)\implies E_{\infty}^{p,q}=\operatorname{gr}^{p}\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}.

Pictorially, this spectral sequence roughly looks like:

Let us check the row
q=n
. By inspection, it must be the case that

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}=\operatorname{gr}^{0}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\Delta_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}.

Note that by tensor-hom one has

\operatorname{\mathsf{R}\text{Hom}}_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}}(L_{\theta}(\theta)^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},\operatorname{\mathsf{R}\text{Hom}}_{\text{N}\cal B}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0}))=\operatorname{\mathsf{R}\text{Hom}}_{{\text{N}\cal B}}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta}\otimes_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}}L_{\theta}(\theta)^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})=\operatorname{\mathsf{R}\text{Hom}}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0}),

and by the Grothendieck spectral sequence, since we have already since
\operatorname{\mathsf{R}\text{Hom}}_{\text{N}\cal B}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0})
is appropriately concentrated, we know that the last equality in the following is true:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hed}\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{n}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}}(\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{n}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0})^{*},L_{\theta}(\theta)^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{\text{N}\cal B}^{\theta}}(L_{\theta}(\theta)^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},\operatorname{Ext}_{\text{N}\cal B}^{n}({\text{N}\cal B}^{\geq\theta}e^{\theta},L_{0}))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0}),

and the head of
\operatorname{Ext}^{n,*}
as we have seen above already is simply
L_{\theta}(\theta)
. Hence

\vbox{}\left.\vbox{\vbox to50.15585pt{\vfil}}\right[\vbox{\vskip-7.5pt\begin{proposition}At $t=0$, letting $\theta=2n$, the nilHecke action on the proper standard Ext groups of the trivial representation is
$$\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{{\text{N}\cal B}}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta}^{\overline{l_{\theta}(\theta)}},L_{0})^{*}\cong L_{\theta}(\theta).$$Equivalently the claim is that as vector spaces
$$\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\text{N}\cal B}(\overline{\Delta}_{\theta},L_{0})\cong\mathbb{C}.$$\end{proposition}\vskip-6.0pt}
